ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u16	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the justification for the bill on the establishment of the National Tax Administration. At the very beginning, the question should be asked at first: Why do you even have the idea of establishing a National Tax Administration? Well, ladies and gentlemen, this, of course, requires a diagnosis of what we have today. Today, what we inherited from our predecessors, we have very low wages in the IRS, so often very low expectations, but we also have a very high VAT gap, we have a very fast growing gray zone and for this reason we have a lot of problems. We'd like this pay to be good, at least decent, to give more tools to the IRS, but also to bet... that there's more expectations. A brief description of what we have today is actually contained in three basic issues, actually four. First of all, a fragmented structure. Tax and customs administrations today have three independently functioning branches: tax chambers, customs offices and tax control offices. As part of the finance department there are 48 units, which are subject to as many as 594 local units. Secondly, insufficient efficiency. Distribution and duplication of tasks related to the collection of tax and customs duties make it impossible to carry them out in a coherent, uniform and optimal use of available staff and organisational and financial resources. Thirdly, problems with the collection of commitments to the budget. The current structure and organisation of services have a negative impact on the level of collection of tax liabilities due. The administrative costs of tax collection in Poland are very high - 1.6% in 2013. For comparison in Ireland this is 0.85% , in Spain - up to 0.67% Finally, the fourth case, the grey zone, the VAT gap, and all the problems involved. Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, as some opponents say today, at the start of this project, you can do nothing. Maybe it's better to just make a cosmetic change. The question is, is it really for the best? It's kind of like that, you could say, like the cake you make. However, it can be considered that without sugar it will also be good and put on the table. Yes, you can. [[We're not talking about tea.]] Yeah, it might look the same. [[The cake must by nature be cute.]] But will it have the same taste? Same here. Without some strategic solutions, system solutions cannot be carried out. [[What about the bakery advice?]] Ladies and gentlemen! It is worth answering two basic questions that are being raised during this discussion. First of all, what is the purpose of creating the KAS, secondly, what will be the organisation of the National Tax Administration after 1 January 2017, of course according to this project. With regard to the establishment of the National Tax Administration, its objectives are aimed at several fundamental issues: strengthening effective tax collection, improving the service and support of the taxpayer, enabling effective enforcement of administrative cash claims, improving efficiency and efficiency in combating tax fraud and the recovery of tax and customs arrears, improving the efficiency and effectiveness of controls, improving the system of financial security of the State, improving cooperation and the exchange of information relevant for the implementation of the tasks related to recognition, detection, combating and prevention of criminal offences, including criminal offences and tax offences. Case two, what will be the organisation of the National Tax Administration after 1 January 2017, because this raises understandable interest, understandable anxiety. The introduction of the National Tax Administration will result in the creation of bodies and organisational units such as the tax administration's office, the tax office and the customs and treasury office. The reform of the National Tax Administration will involve maintaining the current powers of the tax and customs authorities and will not create new obligations for citizens, companies and entrepreneurs, and this should be emphasised. The agencies of the National Tax Administration will be the budgetary units carrying out the tasks imposed on the basis of the annual financial plans. The activities of the National Tax Administration units will be financed entirely from the State budget within the limits of the appropriations for the financial year in question. Could we try briefly to identify what we are proposing when it comes to this reform? In short, there are three basic issues: greater budget revenues, a consolidation and a payer-friendly office for entrepreneurs. I'm gonna start with the first case, more budget revenue. Against the shadow economy today seems crucial to improving state stability and security. Ladies and gentlemen, it is not possible to ignore the fact that today almost 20% all economic activities are carried out in the grey zone. The size of the grey zone is strongly linked to the so-called. a tax gap. According to data from the European Commission published in 2015, the VAT gap in 2013 amounted to EUR 10.13 billion, i.e. 26.7% revenue from this tax. This is, ladies and gentlemen, 3%. gross annual domestic product. This is more than the budget deficit planned for the following year. This is the first case worth leaving. The question is therefore understandable: Will the creation of the National Tax Administration, which is the connection between the Customs Service and the tax administration and the tax control, improve the collection of taxes? Today - this is what it looks like today - each of the services, i.e. tax offices, tax offices and customs offices, has its own separate IT systems, which necessarily hinders the exchange of data. This not only hinders the exchange of data, but also facilitates the action of all kinds of frauds today, because they benefit from some inconsistencies of the system and benefit from the fact that tax authorities often cannot react as quickly as necessary. Reproduction and dissipation of tasks related to the collection of tax and customs duties within individual services, e.g. such as checking the regularity of the implementation of tax and customs obligations, makes it impossible to carry them out in a coherent and uniform manner, while at the same time making optimal use of available staff and organisational and financial resources. The National Tax Administration is to be responsible for carrying out all tasks related to collection of tax and customs duties, starting with the process of registering entities, through handling declarations and other tax and customs documents, checking the correctness of the implementation of tax obligations, clearing and distribution of payments and enforcing outstanding arrears. These single tax administrations at regional level will ensure adequate organisation of work, financial and property resources, and above all the well-trained staff. Ladies and gentlemen, the integration of customs IT systems, tax administration and tax control is also assumed. In other ways, such questions also arise: How fast? What's the deadline? It is natural and obvious that this must be done in a step-by-step manner, by combining systems taking into account technical capabilities, so that this is least felt for taxpayers themselves. The costs incurred in integrating systems will be offset by future benefits both on the taxpayer's side and on the budget side. At present, all these bodies, i.e. tax administration, tax control and Customs, have separate IT systems, which, as I have already said, makes it difficult to exchange data radically and consequently simply force reorganisations in this area. The approach to the analysis of individual areas is adapted to their specifics and IT tools implemented at present in the finance department. Ladies and gentlemen, the introduction of one IT system instead of the current three, the flattening of service units and the reduction of office space rental is of course also not meaningless. Case number two, ordering through consolidation. Ladies and gentlemen, as I have already mentioned, today 48 units are operating under 594 local units. This fragmented structure in three independently functioning verticals causes a number of organisational problems as well as - as we said - related to the effectiveness of the fight against the grey zone or the gap in Vata. The dispersal and duplication of tasks related to the collection of tax and customs duties within the services makes it impossible to make optimal use of staff and organisational and financial resources. Why do we say HR? Because in this connection we also have duplicated structures, I would say, office at all these levels. Thanks to this reform it will be possible to put them on the line, i.e. directly to help taxpayers. The current structure also, as I said, causes very high tax collection costs. Finally, it is worth adding that for 8 years of government of the previous coalition, tax officials had a base frozen at the level of approx. 1870 PLN, while a sharp increase in the minimum wage from 950 PLN, today to 1850 PLN, from next year to 2000 PLN. It was the actions of this department, the Ministry of Finance, that made today the minimum amount of payment in the treasury amount to PLN 2850. In the plans there is an even higher increase, to a level of at least PLN 3000. Finally, why should this be tax-friendly and business-friendly? Of course, someone will say that for some, little will change. Yes, in the worst case scenario, these changes will be neutral for taxpayers, for entrepreneurs. But the introduction of the National Tax Administration does not foresee the liquidation of existing bodies and organisational units. This can often be raised as a threat. The current bodies and agencies will be transformed and then merged. The consequences of these activities will be the creation of bodies and organisational units of the National Tax Administration. At the same time, we can talk about some benefits in this regard. Here, in particular, it is worth mentioning: abolishing rules which differently regulate the control of tax authorities, tax authorities and customs authorities, ensuring the uniformity of policies and interpretations of law in the areas of action by authorities, speeding up tax cases, improving the handling of taxpayers, including the handling and support of the taxpayer and the trader in the proper performance of tax and customs obligations. National fiscal information is important in this respect. Also thanks to national tax information it will be possible to create an email channel. It's also one of the novelties planned. Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! The solutions contained in the draft Act on National Tax Administration will result in the KAS authorities being the Minister of Finance, the head of the national tax administration, the director of national tax information, the director of the tax administration, the head of the tax office and the head of the customs and treasury office. The head of the customs and treasury office of the national tax administration will be able to carry out a customs and treasury check. Customs and treasury control, as so-called. the tough procedure will apply to offences with a high quality weight, e.g. related to the activities of organised criminal groups. Its objective will be to detect and combat large-scale irregularities where the size, complexity and extent of the impact of the deficiencies present have a significant impact on the State's financial security system. Customs and treasury control will therefore be an important tool in reducing the tax gap. At the same time, the draft law assumes the establishment of the Customs and Treasury Service as a uniform and uniform form, which will be created by Customs and Treasury officials. Yeah, I know how much anxiety there is. or how much there is also some kind of resistance on the part of the workers of the present Customs Service. Moreover, discussions on this subject have been ongoing and have been ongoing for several months when we presented plans for the introduction of the national tax administration. [[You're talking to each other.]] I realize that every major change of this kind causes this, otherwise understandable, anxiety. But it should be stressed that, contrary to what we also heard from this speaker before the vote, the aim of fiscal reform is not to eliminate the current uniform formation of the Customs Service, but to rationalise and optimise both employment and the use of existing staff resources in terms of a real assessment of the competences of individual officers. The number of Customs and Customs officials will therefore be appropriate to the tasks they carry out, i.e. primarily the tasks related to the implementation of customs duties and charges related to the transport and export of goods. Within 1 January to 31 May 2017, once again assuming that this law will enter into its unchanged form, the Director of the Tax Administration Board shall submit a written proposal to his staff and customs officers setting out new conditions for employment or service, which shall take into account the qualifications and conduct of the work or service so far as they are concerned, as well as their place of residence. The Act therefore provides for a different regulation than that currently in force concerning the status of an officer. It is assumed that the status of an officer will not be held by persons who do not carry out tasks requiring special powers, which may be performed by civil servants, that is, officials. The status of an officer and the resulting pension privileges and additional leave will be granted only in connection with the performance of police activities in a manner appropriate to these tasks. Other employees will be able to pursue their professional activities using their competences under the National Tax Administration Act, which develops the employment structure of highly qualified workers, especially in the field of judicial tasks. Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to stress this once again: the aim of introducing these provisions, the aim of introducing this reform is not to hit any professional group, the aim is not to bring disaster to anybody, the aim is not to finally see itself as a result of some idea of creating or reforming something forcefully. It's not like that. It is clear that if these bodies, these services and those rules that are in operation today were enough to make a slight adjustment, no one would be throwing themselves into a systemic reform. But we must make it clear to ourselves that today the losses that all Polish taxpayers bear in relation to the regulations that we are dealing with are too big, simply do not allow them to keep their heads in the sand, do not allow this House to continue to be passive, and do not finally allow them to pretend that there is no problem, nor do they allow them to continue, as it was for the previous coalition, to ignore the needs of the tax administration staff as well... [[Don't lie to me.]] ...to leave them without any increase in the performance of not only more difficult, but often also more dangerous duties in connection with the growing of these... it is difficult to call them entrepreneurs, they are simply thieves, criminals who use their business as a screen for their activities. And that's what this reform is going to do. Of course, the bill is quite large, and today we are just beginning to work on it in Parliament. This Parliament is the place where such a discussion should be held, and changes should also be made if necessary. We realize that not everything is completely perfect, that we are waiting for this, as I said, discussion and work. There are allegations of this kind that there has been no introduction, there have been deliberately no provisions specifying the powers of directors. This is an oversight. It's just that. We are going to introduce, we are going to make amendments that would reverse this state of affairs, so we should not assume the wrong will at once, only we can assume that there are certain shortcomings in the course of the work, but today we are starting to work on this law. [[Especially on the knee)]] [[You should have started a few months earlier)]] Not about abolishing, eliminating one of the uniformed services, we are beginning to work on improving the situation and improving the functioning of the customs-credit administration. And I strongly encourage all of you, ladies and gentlemen, to do this work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[President of the session includes Deputy Marshal Joachim Brudziński]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u17	Thank you, sir. I would like to ask Mr Wojciech Murdzk to provide the reasons for the bill in print No. 827. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u18	Dear Marshal, Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The consequence of the regulations provided for in the parliamentary draft law on National Tax Administration in print No. 826 is the need to regulate in a separate law solutions that will ensure effective and efficient implementation of the rules on national tax administration. The Parliamentary draft law The provisions introducing the Act on National Tax Administration from print No. 827 contain both amending, repealing, transitional, adapting and final provisions. The provisions of the National Tax Administration Act, as a general rule, enter into force on 1 January 2017. The introduction of the National Tax Administration will result in the need for amendments adapting to 157 laws. The National Tax Administration will take over the tasks performed so far by tax control, tax administration and Customs, and therefore the provisions of the laws on these formations will expire on 1 January 2017. By virtue of the article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 2 of the proposed legislation, the tax office continues to operate and becomes the tax office. The IRS will be an entity which will have a significant burden of implementing the reform of the IRS. With effect from 1 January 2017, the tax administration will be merged with the customs office and the tax control office. Therefore, the Chamber of Tax Administration, in connection with the merger with the Customs Chamber and the Tax Control Office, enters into all rights and obligations of the merging entities irrespective of the nature of the legal relationship under which those rights and obligations arise. Further changes refer to the Centre for Vocational Education of the Financial Resort, on the basis of which the National Treasury School will be established on 1 January 2017. The draft law indicates that the head of the National Tax Administration is carrying out tasks in the field of the creation and organisation of the National Tax Administration and may issue regulations for this purpose. In terms of staff resources, detailed rules have been adopted for the continued employment and service of officers. These regulations preserve the continuity of work and service. Rules have been laid down for the submission of proposals for continued employment or service. The proposals will take into account the qualifications of the employee or officer, the course of the work or service so far and the place of residence. In accordance with the Article. 169 of the draft law in the event of failure to receive or accept a proposal for employment or service by 31 May 2017, employment relations and professional relations of persons duly employed or serving in tax administrations, combined or abolished entities shall expire and workers and officers shall be entitled to benefits due in connection with the liquidation of the office or the removal of the organisational unit respectively. The following arrangements have been adopted for the continuation of the activities of the National Tax Administration after the reorganisation: in order to maintain liquidity in the performance of tasks and to gradually implement new regulations, without prejudice to the exercise of statutory tasks, pursuant to Article. 183 and Article 183. 186 of the draft law, a three-year period is foreseen for changes concerning: business cards, uniforms, marking of vehicles and vessels and business cars and decisions on the allocation of service weapons. In turn, the provision of the article. 187 of the draft law regulates property issues. The property of the State Treasury and the property at the disposal of the tax houses, customs offices and tax inspection offices shall become the property at the disposal or the permanent management of the local tax administrations respectively. On the other hand, the property of the State Treasury at the disposal of the tax houses and property located in the permanent management of the tax chambers, used by the offices of the National Tax Information in these chambers, become the property at the disposal and property in the permanent management office of the National Tax Information Office. Transitional provisions for the continuation of proceedings and checks - The implementing law lays down rules for the conduct of criminal proceedings initiated and not completed under the existing rules of taxation, taxation, customs, enforcement, protection, judicial and control procedures and other activities, adopting the principle of their continuation by the competent authorities. To that end, the provision of Article shall also be introduced. 234 draft law maintaining all the activities carried out so far within the framework of these activities. Detailed rules for the continuation of proceedings shall be laid down in Article. 195.215 of the draft law. It must be noted that the project was developed and brought to the Sejm in the first half of this year. The dates for certain introductory processes were then scheduled for September and October. Today it is already known that these dates are out of date. In the meantime, a number of new tax laws and some amendments have been adopted, which also require adjustments in connection with the creation of the National Tax Administration. The project therefore requires some updating changes. The regulations contained in the parliamentary draft Rules introducing the National Tax Administration Act, print No. 827, will ensure effective and efficient implementation of the reform of the tax administration. I request that the project be transferred to the Parliamentary Committee for further work. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The Customs Service is based primarily on the Community Customs Code, which is the same throughout the European Union, and the Customs Code law merely complements Community rules in this respect. On the other hand, tax administration, tax control relies its action primarily on the Tax Order and on the substantive provisions of tax law, and UKS - also on the basis of the Tax Control Act. The tasks and conduct of the tax and control authorities are therefore diametrically different from those carried out by the Customs Service at the border of the European Union, and the combination of them into one organism will only bring chaos and consequently budgetary losses. In fact, this will also result in different review procedures in the case of tax proceedings. If we have to deal with proceedings conducted by the head of the customs office, the appeal will be dealt with by the same authority. If the proceedings are conducted by the head of the tax office, the appeal against the decision will be sent to the head of the tax administration. Potentially favourable statutory provisions are the codification of interpretations to be issued by the Director of National Tax Information, the determination of the exact manner in which the tax and customs checks are carried out, the possibility to submit a correction of the declaration within 14 days of receipt of the information on the inspection, the provision of assistance and support in the proper performance of the tax duties by the head of the tax office, the submission of statements of property by some of the employees, the lodging of reservations in writing in a situation where the service order infringes the law or contains an error. The statutory records, which may potentially lead to fraud, concern the possibility of carrying out ad hoc checks only on the basis of a card, the fact that trained officers will have the right to use means of direct coercion, and the fact that officers will be able to conduct searches without the consent of the prosecutor. The head of the National Tax Administration will perform a very special role, which includes: will perform the duties of the Head of the Customs Service, the General Inspector for Revenue Control, the General Inspector for Financial Information, as well as the duties of the Minister for Public Finance so far, in particular to the extent that he supervises the tax administration, will form advisory and advisory teams, decide on staffing and training policies, implement the budget, carry out supervisory and control functions in the framework of the various bodies of the National Tax Administration and officials, carry out audit and certification functions, implement European Union legislation on customs matters, eventually issue regulations. Such numerous powers and duties will require not only a trusted person in this position, as unfortunately happens in the supervisory boards of companies of the State Treasury, but above all competent in many, sometimes very different areas of life. There are doubts about the provisions entering the sphere of civil rights and freedoms, as outlined in the opinion of the National Courts Council. According to Art. 110 of the draft law on KAS, the designer has granted tax authorities the right to obtain, collect, process and use information for the purpose of carrying out tasks which have been defined in a very general way, without indicating the criteria to be met and which would possibly justify the need to exercise the powers conferred on them, leading to significant interference in the sphere of civil rights and freedoms. Such generally defined tasks may give rise to a belief that the principle of proportionality is being infringed, requiring that an appropriate proportion of the employer's interference in the sphere of civil rights and freedoms be maintained and that the result is achieved. A further indication of a breach of that principle is in the assessment of the National Courts referred to in Article. 111 draft law on National Tax Administration allows the collection of information belonging to the individual's privacy sphere only for the purpose of preventing or detecting tax offences. Such determination of the grounds for interference with the rights and freedoms of citizens should be assessed as extremely general and indeterminate. This provides the basis for an uncontrolled collection of relevant information and personal data by the tax administration. The Council also notes that in paragraph 2. 2 of this Article, the designer has imposed an obligation on those collecting information and other important personal data to make it available, limiting the legal grounds for requesting information to be made available to indicate as sufficient only the written request of an officer performing the operational and inspection activities. The projector has therefore created another possibility of violating the principle of proportionality in practice. To sum up, we are counting on leaning on the comments and suggestions of all the communities involved in the Customs and Treasury Department. Thank you. [[Applause]]

In this situation, the question arises: Why this revolution? Is it merely a matter of statutory dismissal, dismissal of workers and employment of only those with specific political sympathies, as was the case in many previous draft laws? Currently existing work relationships according to the project are, in principle, by law becoming labour relations or service relations in the KAS, but their continued existence is subject to the submission by the new body of a written proposal for new working conditions or service. In the absence of such a proposal or its refusal by a staff member or officer, those relations shall cease and the Director entitled to make such a proposal shall be exempted from any obligation to justify his conduct. Staff shall therefore be deprived of the appeal. These are not the only and isolated criticisms of the projects submitted. Negative opinions on the projects were reported by several state institutions, as well as tax advisory companies. Among the opinionaries are the Supreme Court, the National Court of Justice, the National Bank of Poland, the Public Prosecutor's Office and others. The most important points are, among others: interference in the sphere of civil rights and freedoms. Art. The 110th draft law confers on the tax authorities the right to obtain, collect, process and use information in order to carry out tasks which are unfortunately not precisely defined are too general. Art. The 111th project allows the collection of information in order to prevent or detect fiscal offences, thus offering unlimited opportunities to acquire and collect relevant personal information and data. This laconic record of the prevention or detection of fiscal offences is assessed only by an officer performing operational and cognitive activities, as it is this officer who has the right to request information. Art. 112 extends the unlimited capabilities of an officer. Failure to provide information within a period of time or incomplete provision of information shall be punishable by a penalty of up to 10 000. zł, which can be imposed on an institution providing information even the head of the customs office. The project confers prosecutorial powers on the National Tax Administration in the event of the identification, detection and eradication of several types of crime and the prevention of them. It is about organized crime and participation in organised crime, material and intellectual forgery or fraud. These powers will be conferred on the tax administration in cases where those offences have been disclosed by that administration. In its opinion, the National Judicial Council stresses that this is contrary to Article. 49 constitutions. The basic law ensures freedom and the protection of the confidentiality of communication, and their limitation may only take place in the cases set out in the law. The definition of "prevention or detection" is unfortunately not precise. It's a curiosity matter to regulate the use of weapons. Unprecise terms, such as suspicion or prevention, give the possibility to even stop the ship and carry out checks. National Bank of Poland draws attention to art. 48 a project setting out access to banking secrecy. It is to be repealed even at the stage of the preliminary investigation, as well as in the case of fiscal misconduct and not just criminal offences. Art. 60 of the project suggests access to information of the nature of banking secrecy in the situation of any customs and treasury control, i.e. even random control, which falls within the scope of customs and treasury control. The Attorney General raises a number of reservations about the ambiguities of proceedings following the takeover of cases by the new structures. In conclusion, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party identifies itself with the conclusion contained in the opinion of the Supreme Court, which states that the proposed laws should not be the subject of further legislative work. On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I submit a request to reject both projects at first reading. High Izbo, finishing my speech, I would like to warmly greet all customs officers on the day of their holiday, on the day of St. Matthew. The taxes and duties levied by the Customs Service represent 1/3 of the total revenue of the State budget. In 2014, the Customs Service collected and transferred almost PLN 87 billion to the budget, which represents 30% His total income - this is information from the Ministry of Finance. On the occasion of the Customs Day, I wish all customs officers in the country and customs officials, especially those who serve directly at checks and checks. Working in government administration earlier, I had the opportunity to work closely with Customs, securing the border in the Lublin section. I know how important your service is. You stand guard of our European [[♪ The bell ♪]] Customs border. Your service is to ensure economic security not only for our country but also for the whole European Community, which is why your work deserves special recognition. Accept thanks for the professionalism, reliability and courage shown every day. I wish you the success of your personal and professional lives and the continuing satisfaction of your tasks and customs decisions for your formation. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u129	Thank you. Thus, the list of members of the vote has been exhausted. I request the floor of the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Minister Marian Banasi. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u130	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Let me say hello to customs at the very beginning and give them the most heartfelt wishes on the day of the customs official's holiday... [[They're sad.]] ...and I will ensure that 21 September will also be the celebration of customs officers and the entire tax service in the new structure, in the National Tax Administration. Let me start by presenting the government's position on the two projects that have been presented here, and then I will address the questions that have been raised here. The parliamentary bill on National Tax Administration, print No.826, provides for the creation of the National Tax Administration. In the current legal state, the tax and customs administration which collects budgetary revenue within the government has a distributed field structure, which is concentrated in three independently functioning verticals. The tax administration consists of 16 tax houses and 400 tax offices. Customs services are 16 customs chambers, 45 customs offices and 141 customs departments. The tax control is carried out by 16 tax offices and 8 branches. All units at voivodship level have their own budgets, staff and manage movable and immovable property. Tax administration, Customs and tax control also develop separate directions of development, strategic objectives and measures for their implementation. Consequently, the implementation of tax and customs collection tasks is fragmented and inconsistent. They are not optimally used personnel and technical and financial resources. Pieces have independent IT solutions that are not sufficiently cooperative. The lack of diversity of administration results in its suffrage, i.e. its duplication of activities in certain areas and the lack of action or their delay in other areas. All of this has a negative impact on the effectiveness of the actions undertaken by the state administration and the costs of danin collection. Such an assessment of the current situation of the tax administration, fiscal control and the Customs Service indicates the need for a thorough reform of the administrative apparatus. It should be stressed that the need for reform of the tax administration is signalled inter alia.: International Monetary Fund, European Commission services and Supreme Audit Chamber. In a written position of the government on the draft Polish Tax Administration Act, print No. 826, a diagnosis of the current state by these institutions and their conclusions was presented. The draft law on National Tax Administration provides for the establishment of the National Tax Administration in place of the currently functioning: tax administration, tax control and Customs Service. The National Tax Administration will be subordinate to the Minister responsible for public finances. Under the new rules, the national tax administration authorities will be: Minister of Finance, Head of National Tax Administration, Director of National Tax Information, Director of the tax administration, Head of Customs and Treasury Office and Head of the Tax Office. The Minister of Finance will be responsible for coordinating and cooperating in shaping state policy on the tasks of the KAS. The minister's role will also be to identify four-year directions for action and development of the KAS, which will enable the tasks of the KAS to be carried out more effectively and effectively. The head of the National Tax Administration shall primarily supervise the activities of all KAS bodies, shape HR and training policies in the KAS organisational units and implement the state budget within the limits set for the KAS. The Head will be responsible for the tasks currently being carried out by the Head of Customs Service, the General Inspector for Revenue Control, the General Inspector for Financial Information, and the duties of the Minister responsible for Public Finance so far, in particular to the extent that he supervises the tax administration and conducts tax proceedings in individual cases. The Director of National Tax Information will provide uniform and publicly available tax and customs information and will be competent in matters relating to the interpretation of individual tax laws. The Director of the tax administration will carry out to a large extent the tasks currently carried out by the directors of the tax chambers and the directors of the customs chambers and to a certain extent the tasks of the directors of the tax control offices. The head of the Customs and Treasury Office will be able to carry out a customs and treasury check. Customs and treasury control as so-called. the tough procedure will apply to offences with a high quality weight, e.g. organized criminal groups that today act with impunity. Its objective will be to detect and combat large-scale irregularities in situations where the size, complexity and extent of the impact of the deficiencies present have a significant impact on the State's financial security system. Customs and treasury control will be an important tool in reducing the tax gap. The head of the tax office will carry out tasks related to the collection of danin, i.e. the determination, determination, collection of taxes, fees and non-tax fiscal duties, collection of customs duties and other charges related to the import and export of goods, the tasks of the creditor of monetary claims, the administrative execution of monetary claims, tax control and verification activities. The tax authority's task will be to provide services and support to the taxpayer and the payer in the proper performance of their tax obligations, which will consist in providing assistance in the independent, correct and voluntary fulfilment of their tax obligations, carried out in particular by the service centres. Applications and declarations, certificates issued, explanations of tax legislation, including those who are just about to enter into business, as well as information on how to fill in their statements and tax returns will be accepted at the service centres. The bill assumes the establishment of the Customs and Treasury Service as a uniformed formation, which will be created by officers. The status of an officer will be linked to tasks which, due to their specific characteristics, require uniforming and having specific powers, e.g. surgical and cognitive function. The status of an officer will therefore be linked to the execution of police activities. It is assumed that the status of an officer will not be held by persons who do not perform tasks requiring special powers which may be performed by employees or civil servants. Aggressive tax avoidance, tax crime, the grey zone poses serious challenges for our country. The size of the VAT gap poses a serious threat to the security of public finances and reduces the potential of the country's development. Honest taxpayers expect fast and professional service. It is necessary to create a new, efficient and uniform tax system to which the proposed National Tax Administration Act serves. The Council of Ministers supports the parliamentary draft law on National Tax Administration, print No. 826. The next draft law, the parliamentary draft law Rules introducing the Act on National Tax Administration, print No. 827, contains regulations concerning the entry into force of the provisions concerning the National Tax Administration. The Act contains a broad package of adaptations, repealing, transitional and final provisions. The National Tax Administration Act shall in principle enter into force on 1 January 2017. In view of the change in the organisational structure of the tax administration, the Customs Service and the tax authorities in the structure of the National Tax Administration, the project identifies the need for amendments to the 157 laws. In principle, changes in most laws consist in replacing the terminology for tax houses and customs offices with concepts for tax administrations. Similarly, concepts relating to customs authorities are replaced by regulations relating to tax offices or customs offices. Regulations concerning tax control authorities are replaced by regulations concerning customs and treasury offices. In addition, in the existing laws, customs officers regulations will be replaced by customs officers regulations. This is not just a simple, automatic substitution of some concepts. Terminological changes mean that the functions of existing bodies are assigned to new bodies according to the tasks assigned to them by the draft law on KAS. In addition to the adjustment changes, additional changes are proposed, among which a revision of the Tax Order deserves particular attention. The Tax Order shall introduce the tax advisor's institution. The powers of the head of the tax office as the tax authority of the first instance, the director of the tax administration as the tax authority of the first instance and the director of the tax administration as the review body will be conferred on the tax adviser carrying out the judicial activities. Such a new position in the structures of the tax administration will strengthen the expertise of experts who stand out in substance. Ladies and gentlemen, there is therefore no concern that those professionals who are ruling in tax chambers today will have neither employment nor proper place. In connection with the regulations adopted in the parliamentary draft law on the National Tax Administration, the General Inspector for tax control, the Directors of the tax chambers, the directors of the customs offices, and the heads of customs offices shall be abolished. Customs offices shall also be abolished. According to the project, the tax office continues to operate and becomes the tax office. This is a preliminary, extremely important stage of reform. These tax administrations will be responsible to a significant extent for implementing fiscal reform in the field. The key changes are to enter into force on 1 January 2017, when this is connected at the level of each voivodship with the tax administration chamber and the tax control office. The duties and duties of the tax office, the customs office, the tax inspection office shall become duties and duties of the tax administration. The closure balances drawn up by the tax administration, the customs office and the tax control office as at 31 December 2016 shall become the opening balance drawn up by the tax administration as at 1 January 2017. In a similar way, it is proposed to set up the National Treasury School on 1 January 2017 on the basis of the Vocational Education Centre. The proposed law establishes the following bodies of the National Tax Administration: the Head of the National Tax Administration, the Director of National Tax Information, the Director of the Tax Administration Chamber, the Head of the Tax Office and the Head of the Customs and Treasury Office. The following organisational units are also created: National Tax Information, Customs and Treasury Offices and the National Tax Administration Information Centre, which is a state budgetary unit. The project pays much attention to the regulation of staff resources. As a first step, the rules for continuing work or service in converted, combined and abolished units have been laid down until 30 June 2017. Persons employed in tax offices shall become, no later than 30 June 2017, employees employed in tax administrations and shall maintain continuity of work and service. Those persons shall be informed in writing of the place of performance of their duties if they change. The lack of information is equivalent to the performance of the duties at the place of business. Persons employed in combined or abolished units and customs officers serving in these units or in the Ministry of Finance and staff carrying out the tasks of the National Tax Administration employed in the Ministry of Finance shall become, as from 1 January 2017, no later than 30 June, staff employed in the organisational units of the National Tax Administration or Customs and Treasury Officers serving in the National Tax Administration units and shall maintain continuity of work and service. The same rules apply to employees of tax chambers employed before the date of entry into force of the Act on National Tax Administration in tax chambers in Bydgoszcz, Katowice, Łódź, Poznań and Warsaw, carrying out tasks in offices of National Tax Information, and Customs Chamber in Katowice, carrying out customs information tasks. They shall become, no longer than 30 June 2017, employees of the National Tax Information Office. Workers' matters of the Centre for Vocational Education in the Ministry of Finance are also regulated. The law subsequently lays down rules for the submission of proposals for continued employment or service after 30 June 2017. By 31 May 2017, respectively, the Director of National Tax Information, the Director of the Tax Administration Chamber, the Director of the National Tax School and the Director of the National Tax Administration Information Centre shall submit a written proposal to staff and officers specifying new conditions of employment or service. It will be the same for the Ministry of Finance. The Director-General of the Ministry of Finance, in agreement with the Head of the National Tax Administration, shall submit to the employees and officers carrying out the tasks of the National Tax Administration adequately employed or serving in the Ministry of Finance... The Head of National Tax Administration shall submit a proposal to serve officials serving in the Ministry of Finance. These proposals shall take into account the qualifications of the staff member, the officer and the course of his previous work or service, as well as his previous place of residence. In the event of failure to receive or accept a proposal for employment by 31 May 2017, the employment relationship and the professional relations of persons duly employed or serving in tax administrations, combined or abolished shall expire on 30 June 2017. The staff member and the official shall make a statement on the decision to accept the proposal. Failure to make a statement within that time limit shall be tantamount to refusing to accept a proposal for employment or to serve. In the event of failure to accept the proposal, employees and officers shall be entitled to the benefits due in connection with the liquidation of the office or the abolition of the organisational unit respectively. The proposed law contains regulations on the continuation of the activities of the National Tax Administration units after reorganisation and transitional provisions concerning the continuation of proceedings, operations and controls respectively. The rule is to continue the ongoing procedures by the new authorities and preserve the procedural activities of the authorities following the changes made by the reform. In the legislative process, additional adjustments should be made to the extent resulting from recent legislative changes in recent months. The project will ensure a smooth transformation of the three existing separate tax, customs and tax administrations into one coherent and effective National Tax Administration. The Council of Ministers supports the parliamentary draft law on the introduction of the National Tax Administration Act. Mr. Marshal! ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u140	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present a government bill amending certain laws in order to facilitate the eradication of infectious animal diseases, the main objective of which is actually to improve the activities carried out in the fight against infectious animal diseases. The direct reasons for the legislative work are, first and foremost, the need to clarify the legislation in force to combat infectious animal diseases, but also to identify the addressees to whom these rules will apply, to clarify the scope of those addressees. In the course of the eradication of infectious animal diseases, the Veterinary Inspection encountered a number of difficulties. This law amends six laws. The first law to be amended is the Animal Protection Act. Next is the law on the national system of records of producers, the records of agricultural holdings and the records of applications for payment. The third is the Animal Health Act and the fight against infectious animal diseases. Fourth is the Animal Identification and Registration Act. It is also about the Animal Products Act and the Law amending the Animal Health Act and combating infectious animal diseases. High Seym! In the Animal Protection Act, the amendment concerns the possibility for the district veterinarian to order the slaughter of animals which are on holdings which, unfortunately, are not in zones where the animals cannot be moved from those holdings by decision. Therefore, if the host produces in a cycle, say a weekly or a two-week cycle, animals, they grow to him and, in connection with prohibitions and orders, cannot move them, then the district veterinarian will have the possibility to order the slaughter or destruction of these animals for compensation. This has not been possible so far, because this possibility was only in the case of disease control in the outbreak, and here we are talking about areas which are introduced by decisions of the European Commission or by regulations of the voivodships. So there's a lot of ease, because there was actually a general ban on killing in the Animal Protection Act. However, an exception has been introduced here that due to the deterioration of animal welfare, the increase in weight, growth or the increase in the number of living conditions of these animals are deteriorating. Therefore, the district veterinarian may decide to eliminate these animals for compensation. This is a fundamental change. Of course, this change will also facilitate cooperation with farmers, because I do not hide that at the moment when we are dealing with ASF, it is one of the main problems in regions, in zones where sharpenings are introduced. It was the first law and fundamental changes. The second law, on the national system of producer records, the records of agricultural holdings and the records of payment applications, is amended in Article. The 2th bill we're talking about. These amendments are intended to ensure consistency with the Animal Identification and Registration Act. We introduce the possibility of registration in the system of identification and registration of animals of entities that temporarily own animals. So it will be possible to include in this identification system entities that organize trade fairs, shows, exhibitions or through trade in these animals. The second amendment concerns the second law, i.e. the law on the national system... The third amendment, I am sorry, concerns the Animal Health Act and the fight against infectious diseases and will concern the facilitation of the registration of supervised entities by introducing in the system or in the register of supervised entities the identification number REGON or PESEL. Another amendment, a district veterinarian in accordance with that law will have the possibility to determine orders and prohibitions by local law, order the performance of certain obligations to operators, e.g. the hunter will be able to order firing in the county area, the road managers will be able to order the distribution of disinfection mats and the border crossing managers to take certain actions - of course, in return for the actual costs incurred. A similar decision will be made in the case of local law acts issued by the voivode, which include not one county but several counties. So, in short, so far it has been so that the district veterinarian may have introduced by administrative decision certain orders and prohibitions, order the introduction of bans, but only in a campfire, where he fought the disease, and in the nearest protection areas, at risk, but he could not take certain measures outside these areas. That's why we're giving him the opportunity. Speaking of facilitating the fight against infectious diseases, we still mean ASF, but these rules will also apply to other diseases. The district veterinarian will be able to determine how to proceed e.g. With wild boars shot dead, with animals dead, he'll be able to order the delivery of dead or shot wild boars to a specific location. Here we detail the recipient and some of the activities that we expect will be carried out. The bill, which is being amended, does not specify the addressee, so there were very big interpretation problems. These are fundamental changes in the law on the fight against infectious animal diseases. Then we are dealing with a revision of the Animal Identification and Registration Act. There are very important changes here, which are not only forced by the current situation that we have found, and the fact that we have recently been dealing with the spread of African swine fever in an uncontrolled manner, i.e. It was difficult to determine the source. Since the animals were not marked, their movement took place without any documentation. Therefore, the changes concerning the animal identification and registration system will serve to seal the system. Moreover, this is not only due to the situation that occurred during the eradication of African swine fever, but also to the recommendations of the FVO audit and the recommendations of the Supreme Audit Chamber, which indicated that the number of animals in the system of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture and the number of animals indicated by the Veterinary Inspection and also by the Central Statistical Office are simply missing out on each other, and it is difficult to explain. Therefore, this system will first of all be sealed and we will really know what number, here we are talking mainly about pigs, animals are kept in agriculture. What are we changing? First of all, that pigs will have to be registered in the system within 30 days of birth. Any change in their presence, their movement the farmer will have to report within 7 days, and in a situation like now, the ASF threat will be required to report any movements and changes in the stock size within 24 hours. One of the major changes is that the animals, the pigs, will be marked with the numbers of all subsequent flocks in which they were staying. At the moment, according to our regulations, the animal is only marked at the seat of the herd where it was born, and it is difficult to trace on the basis of the data we have, the movement of these animals. This is also a recommendation from the FVO after the inspection last year. Another amendment concerns the proceding, implementation of programmes for the control of infectious animal diseases, which are introduced by Council of Ministers regulations or by a regulation of the Minister of Agriculture, which, in order to be co-financed, had to be approved by the European Commission, except that the European Commission in its decision finally approved such programmes by the end of January and, according to EU legislation, the programme should start, say, on 1 January and it happened in the past that the Salmonella control programme had been in force since January, and it actually appeared several months later. Here we want to avoid these problems, so we will want such a regulation implementing the programme to be possible... to be announced after the European Commission has announced the technical approval of the programmes in November of the previous year.

I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction of Mr Andrzej Bittel to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u189	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present a government bill amending the law on rail transport and some other laws which entered the Polish Sejm on 15 September this year. The draft law was adopted by the Council of Ministers on 19 July. The purpose of the proposed implementation is to implement Directive 2012/34 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 21 November 2012 establishing a single railway area and supplementing the implementation of Directive 2008/57 of the European Parliament and of the Council on the interoperability of the rail system within the Community. Directive 2012/34 governs the functioning of the rail market. The most important aspects are the operation and cooperation of infrastructure managers and railway operators. In addition, the Directive recasts the Directives and combines the provisions of the existing three Directives relating to the development of the Community's railways, the licensing of railway undertakings and the allocation of railway infrastructure capacity and the levying of charges for the use of railway infrastructure. Pursuant to Directive 2012/34, the European Commission has issued six implementing regulations governing matters relating to: access to railway infrastructure for commercial passenger carriers, the rules to be applied when requiring financial guarantees from railway undertakings by infrastructure managers, the licensing of railway undertakings, the rules to be applied when charging for noise costs, the rules for calculating costs directly incurred as a result of train crossing, and the reporting obligations of Member States in the framework of the monitoring of the railway market. The proposed amendments include the Law of 28 March 2003 on rail transport, the Law of 16 December 2005 on the Railway Fund, the Law of 12 January 1991 on local taxes and charges and the Law of 4 September 1997 on the departments of government administration. On the basis of the Act they will be issued by the Council of Ministers or by the Minister responsible for transport implementing regulations on the procedures for carrying out checks, inter alia. procedures for carrying out checks by the President of the Railway Transport Office on the national register of railway vehicles or on the conditions for access to and use of railway infrastructure. In view of the modification of the importance of basic concepts such as railway infrastructure, railway line or railway siding, I would like to stress that the draft law that I am discussing is the biggest amendment of the rules since 2003, in which the Railway Transport Act entered into force. The main changes introduced by the present law concern the financing of rail transport, the government position of the President, the Railway Transport Office, service facilities and the provision of railway infrastructure. Let me discuss these four aspects in more detail. As regards the financing of railway infrastructure, one of the most important elements of the amendment is the creation of legal opportunities for the development of the so-called. a multi-annual maintenance programme. This programme will set out the tasks for infrastructure managers during their lifetime and the resulting financial resources for their implementation. The programme should contribute to the proper maintenance of the modernised network of railway lines. The adoption of the programme will be the fulfilment of Poland's obligations to the European Union and should translate into a measurable improvement in the quality of service of rail customers, both passengers and freight forwarders. However, the improvement in the way rail infrastructure is maintained will depend on the solutions adopted in the programme, which must be agreed within the work of the Council of Ministers. I would like to stress that the successful implementation of the programme's objectives over a period of several years will make it possible to exploit the potential of the national construction industry much wider than at present. This cooperation will also benefit the economy as a whole. The programme will have an impact on the proper maintenance of the modernised network of railway lines. At the same time, investment effects will be developed. I am convinced that the adoption and implementation of the programme will contribute to improving the competitiveness of rail transport and to preparing a more attractive offer for rail customers. Moreover, the implementation of the multiannual programme should exclude the risk of introducing significant speed restrictions or the exclusion from the operation of lower lines, allowing access to work, schools, health centres, etc. inhabitants of towns far from large urban agglomerations, so-called Polish powiatowej. About the need to develop and reduce barriers in the field of district Poland Ms Beata Szydło, Prime Minister of Government, has said many times. Most of the funding for maintenance of infrastructure under the multiannual programme will go directly to the market, thus directly affecting the development of many sectors of the economy. In my opinion, the multi-annual programme will contribute to the development of Polish companies operating in the railway sector, both contractors and subcontractors of repair and maintenance works, as well as manufacturers and suppliers of railway infrastructure elements from various industries, and ultimately national railway carriers and manufacturers of rolling stock. The proposed amendment to the Rail Transport Act amends the supervisory formula by the Minister responsible for Transport over the President of the Railway Transport Office. Currently, the minister responsible for transport is overseeing the President of the UTK, which is a regulatory body within the meaning of the Directive. At the same time, the Minister of Infrastructure and Construction exercises ownership supervision over PKP and PKP PLK companies, for which the President of UTK makes decisions. In addition, according to the Public Collective Transport Act, the Minister responsible for Transport is the organizer of public collective transport in the field of international and inter-voivodeship rail passenger transport, which is also subject to supervision by the President of the Railway Transport Office. The provisions of Directive 2012/34 indicate the obligation to ensure that the railway regulatory authority is independent of other public administrations. Directive 2012/34 is laid down in Article. Article 55 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 that the railway regulatory authority should be legally independent in terms of organisational, functional, hierarchical and decision-making from any other public or private body. Therefore, in formal terms, this situation eliminates the possibility for the Minister responsible for transport to continue supervising the President of the Railway Transport Office. Therefore, the President of the Railway Transport Office will be appointed and cancelled by the President of the Council of Ministers in accordance with the provisions of the draft amendment to the Railway Transport Act. In accordance with Directive 2012/34, a new group of facilities has been identified, so-called. service facilities for the provision of services necessary for the transport process - in particular fuel stations, railway stations, terminal freight stations, marshalling stations, etc. In addition, the obligation to make them available to all railway operators will be introduced. For service facilities, different rules will apply to the provision and charging of service facilities than for railway infrastructure. Whether an object is included in the service facilities shall be determined by its function as defined in the paragraph. 2 and 3 of Annex 2 to the Act, regardless of whether the facility is composed of railway infrastructure. In contrast to the rules applicable to railway infrastructure for the use of service facilities, charges will be allowed to be charged to finance the full costs of the facility, plus a reasonable profit. In addition, a specific case of a service facility was envisaged, such as a passenger station including the railway station, platforms and routes connecting them. The passenger station will be made available free of charge to travellers, while the carriers will be provided with the necessary space for handling travellers and commercial information for payment. As regards the provision of railway infrastructure, the previous Chapter 6 of the Railway Transport Act concerned only the provision of railway infrastructure for train journeys. Due to the need to regulate the provision of access tracks to service facilities, after which maneuvers are usually carried out, the provision of infrastructure for train and maneuvering journeys has been distinguished and numerous amendments and additions have been made to the applicable rules. One of the most important changes is to extend the catalogue of entities that may apply to the railway infrastructure manager by allocating capacity. This right will be conferred not only on carriers, but also on anyone interested in obtaining capacity, including public collective transport organisers. At the same time, in order to limit fraud, the infrastructure manager will be entitled to request financial guarantees in the event of non-use of allocated capacity. Thank you for your attention, I kindly ask you to adopt this regulation as soon as possible. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u191	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Seym! The objectives of this document were to revise and harmonise the three directives of the first railway package in order to simplify, clarify and improve the legal framework for the European rail sector, thereby increasing competition, improving market surveillance and facilitating investment in the rail sector. Already during the work on the Directive at the level of the European Parliament, but also the Council of the European Union, Members of the European Parliament and some Member States, including Poland, drew attention to a number of issues relating to the liability for the deficit borne by railway infrastructure managers as a result of the differentiation of rail access charges, which prefer trains equipped with the European ETCS control system, as well as to the problem of existing broadband lines used exclusively for freight services with unusual technical parameters for the European Union, which combine only one Member State with a bordering third country. This was mainly the case for PKP infrastructure, Wide-Wide-Wide-Wide-Wide-Wide-Wide-Wide-Wheeled Steel Line, LHS, which, according to the package, would have to separate the functions of the carrier and the infrastructure manager. Therefore, the project under examination concerns the opening of the railway market and the standardisation of standards for such transport in accordance with the provisions of Directive 2012/34/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 21 November 2012 on the establishment of a single European railway area. It is worth noting that safety on the tracks is the most important element here. Already today, we are pleased to see a systematic decrease in the number of railway accidents both in Poland and throughout Europe. I would like to stress that during the first term of Poland in the European Parliament and during the second term of office, when I spoke with my previous speaker, Mr Rule, we fought for these packages, we fought for good legislation for Poland. I hope that we will be able to get a lot in committee even now. In the committee, we will discuss changes in the definition and definition of the components of our railway infrastructure, the processes for allocating capacity and charging for the use of infrastructure, the question of determining the competence and independence of the President of the Railway Transport Office, which is important, also the definition of the rules for the functioning and location of our railway stations. Ladies and gentlemen! A smart revision of rail law and the transposition of regulations into rail law are necessary, we all agree with that, but they must take into account the interests of our national companies, and then the competitiveness of this mode of transport will improve, which will help introduce new technologies in our country and, what is important, will improve the competitiveness of entire regions in Poland. Therefore, as a parliamentary club, we will support the referral of this project to further proceding and work to make sure that these solutions contribute as much as possible to the development of this mode of transport. Thank you.

We proceed with the examination of item 6 of the agenda: First reading of the parliamentary draft law amending the Civil Code, Family and Care Code, Code of Civil Procedure and the amendment of other laws (Print No. 730). I ask Mrs Agnieszka to pursue the statement of reasons for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u225	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to represent Members of the Kukiz Club as rapporteur for the bill amending the Civil Code, Family and Care Code, Civil Procedure Code and other laws, print No 730. The bill was submitted through members of the Kukiz club15, but de facto it is a change proposed and advocated by intellectually disabled people, their parents and carers and non-governmental organisations that have been involved in helping people with disabilities for many years. In fact, ten years are waiting for this shift. Its main objective is to ensure that intellectually disabled persons are adequately protected from the negative effects of their deficits while preserving their ability to express their will. The current rules designed to protect people with disabilities from the negative effects of their illness are based on an institution of total or partial incapacity. These two institutions interfere very strongly with the freedom and dignity of persons with intellectual disabilities. For example, because of fear of a dentist or before a regular medical procedure, we often deprive them of the possibility to go to elections, to buy an apartment, to marry, to education or even to set up a bank account. An average of about 2 years is often expected to determine involuntaryness. During this time, there may be a great deal of work in health matters. The institution of incapacitation is primarily a violation of the Constitution, but also of the UN Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities of 2006 ratified by Poland in 2012 and in particular Article. The 12th Convention states that persons with disabilities have the right to be recognised everywhere as legal entities on an equal footing with other persons. At the same time, the State is to take appropriate measures to ensure that people with disabilities have access to the support they may need while maintaining their right to legal action. In recent years, the number of incapacitations has been growing surprisingly rapidly in Poland. At present, it is on average over 60,000. Until a few years ago, there were 20 and 30 thousand of them. Let us add that all institutions, including the Ombudsman, are alarmed that this institution is very often abused. The contested law provides for the introduction of two legal forms which are judicially established: a healthcare professional and a legal guardian for persons with intellectual disabilities. It would be possible to introduce a medical care provider for an intellectually disabled person who is 16 years old if it did not have sufficient discernment, necessary for all medical procedures to save the life or the health of such a person. In accordance with the provisions of the Proceded Law, a medical guardian would establish a custody court at the request of an intellectually disabled person, parents or carers on the basis of a significant or moderate disability decision. The introduction of a legal guardian institution for persons with intellectual disabilities would also be possible if they did not have sufficient discernment in very individual cases identified by the family court. This institution would be intended for an intellectually disabled person over 18 years of age, whose disability would also be confirmed in a judgment of significant or moderate disability. The possibility of setting up a guardian with competence to represent or co-represented is important in these proposals. The project indicates only these two forms of care: representational care - a disabled person has a retained capacity for legal action, and its guardian represents it only in the court's jurisdiction; care with competence for co-decision - which means that the guardian must additionally consent to some of the legal acts indicated by the court. A decision to choose one of these forms or both would be taken by a court depending on the different scopes of legal action. In the proposed amendment, I would like to pay particular attention to the change of terms which are currently in force in Polish law. These are terms like mental retards. or disability. In our project, we replace them for the first time with intellectual disability. The earlier terms in the laws, also in the Civil Code, are clearly misconstrued, and it is they that violate the dignity of the disabled person. This is seemingly a very small change, but for the environment of people with disabilities it represents a big step forward in relation to the way people perceive disability consciously. The changes proposed in the project make it more flexible to protect people with intellectual disabilities and allow them to shape them individually, adapting them to individual needs or situations. Thanks to the new rules, it will be possible to avoid a broad and absolute effect of incapacity, whether total or partial, which is often perceived as stigmatizing unfortunately and, on the other hand, often risked being used by family members not in the interest of an intellectually disabled person. The new legal guardian institution, both with competence for co-decision and representation, will allow courts to limit interference in the decision-making sphere of a person to the minimum necessary, leaving it free to decide on other matters in which it can deal. It will therefore be an institution that protects against the negative consequences of a wrong decision in the area where such a threat occurs and, at the same time, an intellectually disabled person will retain his full legal capacity in the rest of his life. This institution will also allow for parallel degrees of interference depending on the sphere of action - in matters more important (higher risk), a guardian with the right to representation can be established, in matters less important a guardian with the right to co-decision can be established and some cases can be left to the free decision of an intellectually disabled person. In the current legal situation, such flexibility was not possible at all. I would like to add that the solutions proposed in the proposed law have also been based on the principles developed by the Committee on Civil Law Codification - that is very important, I would like to stress - and have received a positive opinion from the National Sejm of People with Disabilities, which brings together more than 150 NGOs who are involved in helping people with disabilities. The opinion was quite positive, and the seymist asks for this project to be adopted. The project was evaluated by many institutions. With regard to the comments made in the various opinions, I would like to point out very clearly at the outset that most of the criticisms of the draft are due to the overinterpretation of the notified provisions. The purpose of the Act is not to introduce new conditions for restricting the freedom of action of disabled persons. The draft law aims to introduce the possibility of using softer forms of legal and medical supervision for disabled persons, which are currently severely restricted by their own participation in legal transactions under the rules on incapacity, whether partial or total. At the first stage of the legislative process, the Sejm Analysis Office issued a negative opinion on the compatibility of this project with the European Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, but the Ministry of Foreign Affairs presented the opposite opinion, arguing that the project was bringing Poland closer to meeting the objectives of this Convention. BAS' opinion was that carers' institutions restrict the rights of persons with intellectual disabilities, but without paying attention to the fact that the current institution of incapacity is a much greater restriction. There were also opinions from the Marshal's Office of the Silesian Voivodeship, which drew attention to the fact that the catalogue of restrictions on legal activities should be rigidly defined. However, in the same opinion it was stated that, given that the subject is specific, there should be considerable flexibility in assessing the situation of a disabled person. So there is a definite contradiction here. The comments also concerned whether the family courts were sufficiently prepared for this kind of ruling. In fact, this argument cannot be the subject of an in-depth discussion, because, as parliamentarians, we have the task of primarily caring for the good, the interests of the citizen, rather than guided by the issue of capacity or failure of institutions or offices. The courts are for people and it is a problem for the courts to adapt their functioning to the tasks entrusted to them by the legislator and which citizens require. The comments also contain reservations about the language used in the project - mainly that it is imprecise. Of course, as I mentioned earlier, this project is not a legal project, it is an environmental project.
