This amendment provides for changes not only in the codes mentioned in the title of the draft, but also in the Act on Competitions of Doctors and Dentists, the Act on Occupational and Social Rehabilitation and the Employment of Persons with Disabilities, the Act on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters and the Act on Patients' Rights and the Act on Patient Rights. The proposed amendment provides for the possibility of setting up a medical care provider for an intellectually disabled person who is 16 years old. The application in this respect could be made by an intellectually disabled person, his parents or a statutory representative on the basis of the indications contained in the disability judgment or a judgment with a moderate or significant degree of disability. For a person who is intellectually disabled who is 18 years old, a legal guardian could be established, as proposed. An application for the establishment of a legal guardian could be made by an intellectually disabled person, his or her relatives in a straight line or siblings. In the event of the establishment of a healthcare professional, the project shall propose that the court permit required today be withdrawn if the patient objects to the examination or provides him with other health benefits. On the other hand, a legal guardian would assist a person under his care in carrying out more important legal activities or represent him as a proxy to the extent specified by the court of custody. Undoubtedly, there should also be a catalogue of actions that guardians will be able to perform by virtue of the Act, because unfortunately, an indication in the Act that in each case the court will individually determine the actions which the guardian will be able to take and which he may not, may cause that the guardian will have to apply to the court every moment to increase his powers, such as the operation of the conclusion or termination of a telecommunications contract, because such a court will have to submit such requests to the court for the extension of his powers, for example. He didn't see it coming. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u238	Thank you. Mr Krzysztof Paszyk, the club of the Polish People's Party, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u239	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I have the honour to present our position on the parliamentary draft law amending the Civil Code, Family and Care Code, Civil Procedure Code and on the amendment of other laws contained in print No. 730. Today's project assumes that the protection of a disabled person will be intellectually provided by the establishment of a legal guardian for the disabled. The powers of the legal guardian shall be determined by the court of custody in a manner proportionate to the needs arising from the person's mental state, provided that the decision restricting his or her ability to act constitutes a definitive measure, only if measures less interfering in the autonomy of the will would not be sufficient to protect his or her interests. The project also assumes that in the case of unauthorised persons, they will be able to request the revocation of the incapacity and replace it with custody by a legal guardian. The legal guardian is to assist the person who is in custody in carrying out the most important legal acts or to represent him as a representative to the extent specified by the court. However, the question arises as to whether the proposed project will respond comprehensively to a problem such as that in June this year. signalled the Ombudsman. He then drew attention to the lack of judicial control over whether a person with a mental disability is to be placed in a social assistance home. The Ombudsman also considered it necessary to carry out regular checks to verify whether it was appropriate to extend the stay in those social services centres. In 2012, the European Court of Human Rights in Strasbourg dealt with the Polish citizen's complaint about the impersonalisation and inclusion of him in the DPS. The Court found that a man was capable of expressing his opinion on his situation and that putting him in a home of social assistance, which completely controlled his treatment, movement and invalidity pension, was a deprivation of liberty in violation of the Convention on Human Rights. Unfortunately, there are no explicit provisions in the proposed bill on this issue, and it is a pity, because this could also be included in the proposal. The project indicates two forms of care: representational care, leaving the beneficiary with legal capacity to act while giving the guardian the authority to represent the subject in the designated scope, and co-decision with competence to reserve the consent or confirmation of the guardian for the validity of the designated types of legal activities of the beneficiary. The analysis of the project raises the question whether this range of legal solutions should not be extended due to the varying degree of intellectual disability of persons to whom legal guardians will be granted. In particular, care with competence for co-decision should be subject to the degree of disability and not to the same formula for all. This bill introduces a new kind of care, that is, a healthcare provider. According to the project, if the patient objects to the study or receives other health benefits if the patient is the person for whom the healthcare professional is established, the consent of the caregiver will not be required. In turn, the family aspect of the law should be regarded as beneficial. As a general rule, parents should be appointed as a legal guardian and as a healthcare professional, and if this is not possible, the care provider should be established from among relatives or other persons close to the caretaker. In our view, such a solution will make it easier for families to take care of the disabled. Mr. Marshal! Honourable ladies and gentlemen, The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party in spite of the shortcomings, which includes the proposed project, proposes to refer it [[♪ The bell ♪]] for committee work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u259	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! It will not be the first time in this Chamber that the idea of the project is right. Specifically, as regards the doubts on the part of the Ministry that I represent, it is about posting the proposed provision of the Article. 175 § 1 in the Family and Care Code. At the same time, the amendment of the article. 16 Civil Code duplicates the same solution. Such an approach confuses the transparency of the proposed amendment, in particular as the institution for setting up a legal guardian may result in a limitation of the legal capacity of the person for whom it has been established and is very similar to the current partial incapacity. That was also said several times today. It seems necessary to regulate both of these institutions in a single provision or in neighbouring legislation while identifying the differences between them. Come on. According to the proposed Article. 175 § 1 of the Family and Care Code, the legal guardian would represent the subject as a proxy and not as a statutory representative, which is contrary to the wording of the proposed article. 175 § 3 paragraph 2 of the family code, which provides for the possibility for the guardian court to reserve the obligation to consent or to confirm by the guardian for the validity of the legal acts indicated, in line with the current solution in Article. 18 § 1 Civil Code. It should also be pointed out that the draft provides for the introduction of the proposed article. 15 The Civil Code of an Unknown Civil Law of an Institution with limited legal capacity, which would result from the establishment of a legal guardian by a custody court, contrary to the principle set out in Article. 8 § 1 k.c., according to which every person has legal capacity since birth, and its limitation is possible only by means of a judicial decision on total or partial incapacity and depending on whether the person concerned is unable to direct his or her own conduct or needs only assistance to conduct his or her affairs. This is something that Mr Buda has just mentioned. Solutions for so-called The health care provider also appears not to meet the declared objectives, are insufficient and inconsistent. A medical guardian would be established only for persons aged 16 years or over who are intellectually disabled and do not have the necessary knowledge to agree to the examination or to provide other medical services. At present, parents are, in principle, the statutory representatives of a child under their parental authority, i.e. It's usually until you're an adult. The introduction of an additional care institution for persons aged 16 and over does not seem appropriate. Currently, consent is needed for the provision of medical services for a minor aged 16 years old and for an intellectually disabled person. If you object if you are at the same time ill mentally or intellectually disabled, but with sufficient discernment, it is possible to provide medical services after obtaining the consent of a statutory representative or guardian of the facts and additional court clearances. The medical guardian in the proposed version would replace the statutory representative and the court, denying the right to a decision of the person he is caring for. However, the justification for the project does not suggest that such decisions of this healthcare professional can be verified. Next - the last group of doubts about the procedure for setting up a healthcare professional or a legal guardian. The lodging of an application to the court will depend on the prior receipt of a disability decision by an intellectually disabled person for whom such a guardian would be established. The concept of intellectual disability is not present in the current legal order. The amendments proposed in Article are not justified. 5 of the Act of 27 August 1997 on occupational and social rehabilitation and employment of persons with disabilities. District disability assessment teams operating on the basis of the above mentioned above. the laws do not categorize persons because of the type of disability, by merely assessing the functional limitations underlying the judgment. On the one hand, the proposal to indicate in a disability decision that an intellectually disabled person meets the conditions of non-recognition could stigmatize such a person and, on the other hand, discriminate against persons who do not have sufficient discernment to agree to carry out the study or to provide other health services and are not intellectually disabled persons. Last but not least, this statement cannot be accepted either that the proposed solutions do not have any financial implications for the budget, since, firstly, the basis for establishing a medical or legal guardian is to be a judgment, together with an indication of what may result on the part of persons already having a disability decision in the form of a re-application for the status of a disabled person due to a change of legal status and the absence of such indication, and therefore, in the case of persons with a previous judgment, there will be no possibility of setting up a medical practitioner and they will be forced to re-trial, which will entail difficulties and costs. The scale of the persons concerned may be significant, as a total of 110 098 decisions on the cause of disability were issued in 2015: mental impairment, mental illness or partial developmental disorders, and 392 944 such decisions in 2008 and 2015. To sum up, High Izbo, the formal position of the government is not yet in place, but the project is being prepared in the Ministry of Justice, is a draft negative position towards this parliamentary bill. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u263	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Senate bill aims to amend the article. 647 § 5 of the Act of 23 April 1964. Civil Code in such a way that this provision makes it clear that the investor and the contractor are jointly and severally liable for the payment of the remuneration for works carried out by the subcontractor, provided that the investor agrees in writing to conclude a further contract for works between the contractor and the subcontractor. High Izbo! The main objective of this project is to eliminate situations that have been very often encountered recently, i.e. the investor commissioned the work of the contractor - mainly construction works, but this mainly concerns road works and others, where the general contractor was a capital company - often it was a several-person company with a desk and a place, it won the tender and commissioned all these works to subcontractors. It was often the case that the investor did not even know that the main contractors were subcontractors of this work, even though the contract had been concluded with the main contractor. There wouldn't be anything out of the ordinary if it weren't for the fact that there was often such a practice that the contractor didn't pay or pay the subcontractors the money they owed them because there was no collateral. We propose in this law, you could say, increasing the role of the investor, securing the investor, it might look that way. Except that, respectable Izbo, an investor, when concluding a contract with the contractor, accepting in writing the employment of subcontractors, assumes responsibility and at the same time eliminates from the market the unreliable companies, several companies that win tenders. Why? Because the contractor, knowing that he will be able to make a deal with the investor, will not be exposed to the fact that the main contractor who won the tender, because it is necessary, will fool him. This is all included in our projects. Finally, it is the informed decision of the investor to conclude a contract with subcontractors. We also want to make it possible for the investor to have a choice. Sometimes he can hire a smaller company, but the genuine company that provides services on the market, not a company that, through contractors, often makes such contracts with the awareness that he will not pay, maybe not to the first subcontractor, but to the next few. That is why it is a request to the High House to adopt a Senate bill in the form we have proposed. Thank you very much.

The necessity to meet these two conditions was about to protect the investor from double payment, because after consent he could control the payments and possibly suspend payment to the main contractor. From the beginning, this provision has created huge problems of interpretation, but it must be said - as is the case in our legislative situation - that first there is not a fully coherent and good law, and then the courts are tired and trying to correct the situation in the course of case law. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u275	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Before I begin, Mr President, thank you very much for these positive words to the judges. The project shall be modified by adding to the article. 647 § 5 of the sentence, the importance of which is expressed in the limitation of the investor's joint and several liability for the payment of remuneration for works carried out by the subcontractor in cases of expressing written consent to its execution under the conditions of nullity. The new wording contradicts the case law of the Supreme Court for the last 8 years and introduces an investor protection instrument which can easily turn against subcontractors, often economically weaker. It limits the scope of joint and several liability of the investor. This provision serves to strengthen the sub-contractor's position by having an investor's own interest in ensuring that the subcontractors' remuneration is paid, otherwise it may be forced to satisfy them with its own resources. The position of the investor vis-à-vis the contractor allows him to enforce his obligations to subcontractors, e.g. by making the payment of part of the remuneration corresponding to the remuneration of the contractor subject to proof that the subcontractor has been paid. Despite the strengthening of the sub-contractor's position, the examination of Article. 647 k.c. leads to the conclusion that the investor has not been deprived of the necessary protection by the obligation to obtain the investor's consent expressed by making a declaration of intent or the expiry of a period of 14 days until it has been lodged. The possibility of obtaining knowledge of the content of the contract between the contractor and the subcontractor, which is intended to provide sufficient safeguards for the investor's interests in the course of reconstructing his will, is to be understood as providing him with a real opportunity to become familiar with the provisions of the contract which are relevant to his responsibilities. Real opportunity means a state in which only the investor's will depends on whether or not he becomes acquainted with the content of the contract. The proposed regulation contradicts the resolution of the Supreme Court of 2008, which harmonised the case law on investor consent. The Court of First Instance held that the consent required by the Article. 647 § 2 c.c. the standard Article shall not apply. 63 § 2 k.c. The annulment of the instrument of consent gives rise to concern. 2, if the written form ad solemnitate is not maintained. The nullity of the consent does not prejudge the nullity of the contract itself concluded with the subcontractor. Consequently, such an agreement, which was concluded despite formal disagreement, is important irrespective of the rules on joint and several liability. The legislator did not identify the legal consequences of refusing written consent, although this could be an instrument of impact on the weaker, which in fact led to a return to the state before the amendment of the Civil Code in 2003 and to the previous case-law line. The case law of the Supreme Court, in this regard, has been the subject of extensive research by the Institute of Justice. They give an image of a uniform and consistent position on the form of the investor's consent from § 2 and its economic function. The proposed amendment will substantially shift the centre of gravity from the protection of subcontractors towards the investor. It follows from the justification of the project that its objective is to eliminate cases in which the investor is liable to subcontractors despite the performance of the entire undertaking to the general contractor. The solution proposed in the project does not eliminate the possibility of such a double burden on the investor as it retains the general principle of its joint and several liability to the contractor for payment of the contractor's remuneration for construction works. The argument raised in the explanatory memorandum that the investor's liability extends to all obligations towards the subcontractor, regardless of the state of knowledge of the work carried out by the subcontractor, is not confirmed either in case law or in doctrine. The effects of the proposed amendment in § 5 are unclear in terms of the liability of the contractor and subcontractors towards further subcontractors. The system interpretation, the introduction of a written form requirement for the investor's consent in § 5, and not in § 2, argues that the requirement of written form applies only to the investor's consent. On the other hand, the liability of contractors and subcontractors to further subcontractors would remain unchanged. For these reasons, the Modern requests that the draft law be rejected at first reading. I would also like to draw attention to one thing, namely that the Ministry already stated in the opinion that it is working on the amendment of the Civil Code, among others. over this recipe. Therefore, we will proceed twice: now and in time, [[♪ The bell ♪]] When the Ministry reports it. Ladies and gentlemen, a waste of time and money for the taxpayer. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u277	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club to present our position on the Senate bill amending the Civil Code Act, contained in print No. 768. A draft amending the article. 647 § 5 of the Civil Code provides that the investor should be jointly and severally liable for payment of the remuneration from the contractor only if he has agreed to conclude a further contract for works in writing under the obligation of nullity. The authors of the project indicate that the amendment aims to increase investor control over contracts concluded with subcontractors, which is directly translated into joint and several liability. At the same time, the creators justify that, in the current legal state, the investor's consent to the works by subcontractors is often presumed to be acceptable to the construction site by means of a silent acceptance of their presence. This favours the abuse by contractors of contracts for further works without the express consent of the investor. It should be noted that the current provisions of the Civil Code favour subcontractors and deprive investors of any protection against the obligation to pay for third parties' obligations. Subcontractors often do not receive works payable to them by contractors, generally large capital companies. Then they go with their claims to the investor, even though he transferred funds to the contractor. This results in a situation where an investor is obliged to pay twice for the same job. Moreover, the investor is deprived of the instruments to defend against unfair subcontractors who can freely determine the prices of contracts without his knowledge. It opens up a field for fraud and fraud. In conclusion, the current legislation significantly undermines the legal balance, significantly strengthening the status of the subcontractor vis-à-vis the investor. It should be stressed that investors are not only private entities. Often the problem of failing contractors and unpaid subcontractors is experienced by local governments and, above all, housing cooperatives, whose representatives have particularly appealed and called for the amendment of this law. In our opinion, limiting the investor's co-ownership to the situation in which he agreed in writing to entrust part of the subcontractors' work is an attempt to civilise the under-defined so far. relations on the line investor - contractor - sub-contractor and increase transparency of these relations. In our opinion, however, it is worth paying attention to the opinion of the National Cooperative Council, which concerns the project that we are proceeding with. According to this opinion, the proposed amendment may not be sufficient if it is not linked to the amendment of the article. 647 § 2 of the Civil Code. This provision still includes the construction of a legal presumption that by silent consent the investor accepts that the general contractor uses the services of subcontractors, which may lead to further interpretational doubts. Despite numerous reservations about the Senate project as a club, we are in favour of transferring it to committee work and, on the basis of the results of this work, we will decide to support the final product, this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u278	Thank you. Małgorzata Zwierzan, Circle of Members of the Free and Solidarni will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u279	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Senate draft law amending the Civil Code Act. In 2003, after a series of abuses, which many subcontractors, mostly small family businesses, led to bankruptcy by failing to pay their work due, the Civil Code of Art was introduced into the Act. 647. The records of this article were intended to safeguard the interests of subcontractors who, in the absence of any remuneration due to them from their contractors, were able to claim their fees from investors. In addition to the joint responsibility of the investor and the contractor, the said article introduced an institution of implied agreement of the investor to conclude a construction contract with a subcontractor by the contractor. As the projectors and petitioners point out, whose demands are to implement these proposals for regulation, solutions aimed at protecting subcontractors have, at the same time, begun to impose additional risks on investors. In the case of certain situations, they are obliged to bear the costs twice: by paying the amount first to the unfair contractor and later to the victim of the subcontractor. The contested bill raises a lot of controversy. According to this solution, the investor would be jointly and severally liable if he gave his written consent to the employment of subcontractors and if he gave his consent silently that liability would not apply. According to the opponents of this amendment, the obvious fact is that if an investor is given the opportunity to be free from joint and several liability by not giving written confirmation to the subcontractor, it will benefit from this possibility and in practice such solidarity will not arise. This translates into a renewed lack of protection for subcontractors who, without a guarantee of receiving money for their work, will increase the cost of funding. According to the supporters, this novel aligns the market positions of the investor and subcontractors, making positive changes both for one and the other. This is a clear signal for the subcontractor that if he wants joint and several liability to be in force, he must obtain the investor's written consent. For the investor this is the introduction of additional protection, which reduces the risk of investment. While not agreeing to any of the arguments put forward in its opinion, bearing in mind that further work on these regulations is needed in order to find the best solution, the Free and Solidarni Circle will vote in favour of further proceedings. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u288	Thank you. The list of Members registered in the vote has been exhausted. Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice Mr Łukasz Piebiak will speak. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u289	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! According to the current wording of the article. 647 The civil code shall be jointly and severally liable to an investor, contractor or subcontractor vis-à-vis a non-direct counterparty on condition that any agreement to conclude a construction contract with that entity is given in any form, and in the event that the investor, contractor or subcontractor is presented with a sub-contractor - possibly a further subcontractor - or its design, together with a relevant part of the documentation, no objection within 14 days shall mean the agreement specified in the doctrine as silent or passive consent. The silent consent provided for in Article must be distinguished. 647 § 2 gd 2 c.c. from the implied consent provided for in Article. 60 of the Code, i.e. any behavior of a person who reveals his will in a sufficient manner. The agreement is implied by the form of active consent. It consists in expressing its will in a way other than verbally, i.e. other than in writing or orally. Against the background of the current wording of the article. 647 Civil Code controversy and divergences in case law relate primarily to implied consent. The silent agreement does not give rise to any problems in case law, but, with regard to the agreement of the implied caselaw, it has not developed uniform criteria to consider the investor's behaviour as consenting to the conclusion of a contract with a subcontractor. In extreme cases, the investor shall be attributed an implied consent only on the basis that the supervisory inspector knew about the performance of the works by the subcontractor concerned, while in case law dominates the view that the investor's consent sanctiones all other requirements necessary for the investor's responsibility towards subcontractors. Assigning an implied consent to an investor, in situations where the investor did not realise that he had agreed, led to the investor's joint and several liability for the remuneration of subcontractors in the absence of awareness of the existence of that liability. Consequently, the investor, not realizing the claims of subcontractors and his liability for these claims, could have settled with the contractor without verifying whether the contractor had paid to its subcontractors. Because Art. 647 does not restrict when subcontractors submit their claims to an investor, subcontractors sometimes report them only after the investor has cleared the investment and paid the contractor the full remuneration. Then the investor may be forced to pay subcontractors for the works for which the contractor has already paid for, and regressive claims against the contractor may prove irrecoverable. The absence of time limits for the submission of a sub-contractor's claims to the investor results from the assumption that the investor knows which contracts he has granted active or silent consent to, and thus checks before paying the contractor's remuneration whether the contractor has paid to his subcontractors. However, recognition as an implied agreement of too wide a range of investor behaviours makes this a fictional assumption and results in investors sometimes paying for the same robots once again. The limitation of the form of consent to the written amendment will eliminate the risk of double payment unless the investor does not check the satisfaction of the subcontractors' claims on which the agreement was given prior to payment of the contractor's remuneration. High Izbo! It cannot be made clear that the proposed change leads to a weakening of the protection of subcontractors, as was discussed in today's discussion. Introduction of Article 2003 647, although intended to protect subcontractors, could in the long term have contributed to reducing this protection. Before the entry into force of the Article. 647 of the Code Contractors were aware of the importance of securing their interests in the event of the insolvency of the Contractor. The often used security was the assignment of the right to part of the remuneration for the subcontractor. Introduction to the c.c. That's the art. 647 weakened the vigilance of subcontractors and it should be stressed that it is still alive - the project does not concern it, does not abolish - the right of the subcontractor to require the contractor to provide a bank or insurance guarantee of payment under the strict conditions of withdrawal for reasons incriminating the contractor, i.e. Article. 649 Civil Code. In this situation, with a view to strengthening legal certainty and avoiding liability, avoiding situations where there is double satisfaction, often as a result of criminal acts - various forms of collusion operate on the market in order to ultimately ensure that the same work is paid twice, including often a public investor - we consider that not always every investor should be protected, including the public. It is this project, in writing, that has the chance to prevent such negative phenomena. Therefore, in the assessment of the Ministry of Justice, as well as the Ministry of Infrastructure, because the government's position has not yet been formally adopted by the Council of Ministers, the project is worth supporting. It only requires a minor modification - that it is not justified to introduce written consent as a condition for the investor's responsibility towards subcontractors, while leaving the existing general liability of the contractor to further subcontractors, i.e. providing for liability also if consent is implied and that silence is accepted for consent. If the Senate initiative had been adopted, there would have been a situation where an investor would have been in accordance with Article. 647 § 2 c.c. could agree to the performance of works by a sub-contractor in a form other than written, but at the same time this agreement, in the light of the new wording proposed by the Senate. 647 § 5 k.c., would not give rise to the investor's liability for the remuneration of the subcontractor. Therefore, the objective of the Senate project, it seems, can be achieved in a simpler way by leaving unchanged § 5 and the requirement of written consent in § 2, which should be stated that the Contractor shall require the consent of the investor in writing to conclude the contract of works with the Subcontractor. Failure to make a written statement of consent within a period of not less than 14 days shall be deemed not to have been granted. In addition to the elimination of non-legal consent, the above proposal retains the uniformity of the investor's liability to subcontractors and contractors vis-à-vis further subcontractors. The proposal provides for the withdrawal of the requirement to join the sub-contractor's declaration with the subcontractor or its design together with part of the documentation relating to the performance of the works specified in the contract or project. This requirement was necessary to consider the investor's silence as consent, but since the investor is to grant his consent only in writing, it is for him to decide on the submission of such documents to be subject to his consent. Therefore, according to the Ministry of Justice, the project is worth supporting, yet overworked and refined in committee. I would like to stress that this is not the final regulation of all the problems and even the pathologies that occur in the construction market, this is the first step, but this is a step in the right direction, which is why we support this Senate initiative. There will be time for further steps. The preliminary work is really taking place, this is the work of the Ministry of Justice, together with the Ministry of Development and the Ministry of Infrastructure. And it's not like Marshal Dolnyak here said it was a waste of time. It is not a waste of time to introduce good solutions as soon as possible, but Kraków was not built immediately. In six months, a year later, solutions will be introduced to complete and eliminate all pathologies. I think it's worth at least part of the pathology to eliminate. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u299	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Government's draft law on out-of-court resolution of consumer disputes, print No 630. The Government's draft law is a purely technical project and aims at implementing the new Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council, the ADR Directive of 2013. This Directive aims to harmonise the system of out-of-court dispute settlement between consumers and traders throughout the EU market, including on contracts concluded via the Internet and cross-border contracts. While the ADR system is currently in force in Poland, it is based not on hard legal bases, but on self-regulation and rules of universal law, and on the recommendations of the European Commission regulated by 1998 and 2001 documents, i.e. the 1998 recommendations and the 2001 recommendations, in which the Commission introduced minimum standards for ADR procedures and which are to be the basis for Member States. The first reading of the project took place at a joint meeting of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on 6 July this year. A subcommittee was set up five times, the first meeting on 6 July being formal and concerned the formation of the subcommittee. The next, 20 July, was both a general consultation with the environment and concerned the presentation of the principles of work on this project because - as I have already pointed out - there is no experience of the law in Poland and that it is an implementing directive, and here the European Union is using quite strict methods of verifying this implementation, the point is that all the provisions or all the recommendations of the directive are actually included in the draft law. There's some understanding here. This project was burdened with many, many reservations. It must be said that this project, although the government has been working on this project for a long time, is a good reflection of the provisions of the directive for 3 years, but it also has shortcomings related to compliance with the Polish legal system and the principles of legislative technology. And here the main task belonged to the Legislative Bureau and the UOKiK. Their representatives met between meetings to find good solutions. The main objective of the project is to provide consumers with the possibility to apply for the settlement of disputes with traders to operators offering independent, impartial, transparent, effective and rapid alternative solutions. As envisaged in the legislative package, access to cheap and efficient ways of resolving consumer disputes will be beneficial both for consumers and for entrepreneurs who will obtain an instrument to avoid long-term and costly judicial processes in disputes with consumers. Although there is a constant upward trend in Poland towards amicably resolving consumer disputes, the mechanisms for amicable settlement of consumer disputes in Poland are still too small and widespread. However, as the reality shows, arbitration courts and other forms of out-of-court consumer dispute settlement are an effective and inexpensive means for consumers to exercise their rights, in particular in minor cases, in cases where the general judicial system does not work because of the length of proceedings, high costs, formalised procedures and psychological resistance to consumer involvement in legal proceedings. The result of the adoption and entry into force of this project should be: improving the efficiency and efficiency of procedures related to the amicable resolution of consumer disputes in Poland, introducing uniform methods of acting of entities and ensuring the consistency of the whole system, popularising this form of dispute settlement among Polish entrepreneurs and consumers. In addition, it should be noted that this project is not intended to impose on out-of-court consumer dispute resolution entities ready-to-use solutions, but is intended to create a framework which will, on the one hand, allow the relevant authorities to enter in the event of bad or defective activities of the entities, on the other hand, will give the dispute resolution entities a large amount of freedom by creating their own rules of operation. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law on out-of-court resolution of consumer disputes, contained in Parliamentary Printing No 630, together with the amendments adopted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u302	Thank you very much. Mr Antoni Mężyłko will now speak on behalf of the Civic Platform Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u303	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club the position of the club on the report of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Government's draft law on out-of-court consumer dispute resolution. The government proposal for a law on out-of-court consumer dispute resolution is a purely technical project aimed at implementing the new Directive of the Parliament and of the ADR Council of 2013, which is the Directive on alternative methods of resolving consumer disputes. With the same date, a new regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 2013 was published in the ADR package, namely the regulation on the online consumer dispute settlement system, which in the ADR package amends the old 2004 ODR Regulation and the old 2009 ADR Directive. In view of the growing integration and harmonisation of the internal market in the European Union in all economic sectors, this new ADR-ODR package aims to provide consumers with better access to independent, impartial, transparent, effective and rapid alternatives to consumer dispute resolution. It is also beneficial for entrepreneurs as it provides them with access to cheap, efficient ways of resolving consumer disputes as opposed to costly litigation. The ADR Directive provides for a single system of out-of-court dispute settlement in all Member States which covers all disputes on the EU market between the consumer and the trader, including contracts concluded via the Internet and cross-border contracts. In Poland, there is currently an ADR system based on self-regulation and rules of universal law and international standards, in particular the recommendations of the European Commission regulated by 1998 and 2001 documents, in which the European Commission introduced minimum standards for ADR procedures and which are the basis for Member States to notify the European Commission of national out-of-court entities meeting these criteria. Currently, the following systems are notified to the ADR database in Poland and the European Commission: bank arbiter, arbitration court at the President of UKE, arbitration court at the Financial Supervision Commission, ADR proceedings conducted by the Financial Ombudsman and mediation conducted by the Trade Inspection. The ADR system in Poland is based on two pillars. The first is general and multisectoral and the second is sectoral. The first form the structures of the Trade Inspection and includes mediation conducted by voivodship trade Inspection Inspectorates and amicable judiciary, within which 16 permanent friendly consumer courts and 15 out-of-town centres operate. The second pillar is composed of public administrations, the President of the Office for Electronic Communications, the Financial Supervision Commission and the Financial Spokesperson, and ADR entities set up by businesses in the industry, such as the Bank Arbiter or the Commission for the Protection of Passenger Rights at the President of the Civil Aviation Authority. The Government's draft law on out-of-court consumer dispute resolution introduces a new model of ADR system in which both non-public and public ADR entities will exist. It will include ADR entities set up by businesses in the industry, existing or newly established public sector ADR entities located in or within the structure of public authorities, sectoral administrations such as UKE President, URE President, UTK President, Financial Supervision Commission and Financial Spokesperson, and horizontal entity, Trade Inspection, the scope of which will cover cases for which the relevant sectoral ADR has not been established. This project has been created for a long time, because for 3 years, and although it reflects all the provisions of the directive well, it has many shortcomings in the principles of legislative technology and synchronization or compliance with the Polish legal system. Many of the provisions contained therein do not meet the conditions of tough legal standards and are only a faithful translation of the Directive. Therefore, in the work of the sub-committee set up for the examination of the project, we have carried out a real legislative revolution, introducing about 500 amendments, mainly legislative-editorical-language changes, but also about 50 substantive and structural changes resulting from the principles of legislative technique, consisting in changing the order of chapters. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As a result, in the report of the Joint Parliamentary Committees we have a good project, which faithfully implements the provisions of the Directive, fits well into the Polish legal system, is fully in line with the principles of legislative technique and deserves the support of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club. We're gonna vote anyway. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u307	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Madam President! This is indeed a good project, because we are all aware of how the common courts are burdened. This proposal, as has already been said, follows from the 2013 EU Directive. This new ADR model is to be based on a mixed approach in which both non-public and public ADR entities exist side by side. In order to obtain the status of an entity authorised to settle consumer disputes out of court, they must be entered in the register of entities authorised to be held by the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. The Act also applies to proceedings aimed at out-of-court resolution of domestic and cross-border disputes concerning contractual obligations arising from sales contracts as well as other contracts concluded with consumers by traders established in the territory of the Republic of Poland. The project sets out the requirements that are necessary for the management of an ADR entity. The basic principle of dealing with out-of-court consumer dispute resolution is to initiate this procedure at the consumer's request, but the ADR entity will be able to provide in its internal regulations for the possibility of initiating ADR proceedings also at the trader's request. The proposed ADR system is based on a voluntary principle that ADR procedures should be voluntary for parties and should not close the judicial path. The Modern Club recommends voting for this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u310	Thank you very much. Mrs Małgorzata Zwierzan will now speak on behalf of the Free and Solidarni wheels.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u311	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I am pleased to present my position towards the government's draft law on out-of-court resolution of consumer disputes. The draft law is based on the need to implement the legislative package, which was published on 18 June 2013. The legislative package is intended to enable the proper functioning of the internal market in the European Union and to ensure that consumers resolve disputes with traders without the participation of a court. Such a solution is beneficial for both consumers and entrepreneurs, who have so far wasted unnecessary time and incurred high costs in litigation. So far there is a mixed model of the out-of-court dispute resolution system in Poland. The system is based on two pillars: the Trade Inspection covering the whole country, among others. the mediation carried out by the provincial trade inspection inspectors, and by the arbitration courts. The second pillar forms the so-called. sector-specific ADR entities set up to resolve specific disputes in specific market sectors. They use mediation, arbitration or both to resolve disputes. These solutions were rarely used by Polish citizens. The scope of the draft law concerns consumer disputes involving contractual obligations arising from sales contracts and other contracts concluded with consumers. The draft law introduces rules to ensure fair treatment of the parties when resolving consumer disputes. During the work of the subcommittee, a fragment of the article raised a lot of controversy. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and 2 concerning the online and non-internet form in which consumer dispute proceedings may be conducted. This passage also raises our doubts. The Act introduces a very important regulation which ensures that the limitation period for a claim which is the subject of a dispute will not expire during all types of proceedings in the case of out-of-court consumer disputes. It should also be stressed that the proposed method of out-of-court dispute settlement does not close the road to legal proceedings. The Parties shall choose on a voluntary basis how to settle a dispute. The Act also sets out clearly the obligations of ADR entities as well as traders. The project sets out additional tasks for the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, including: monitoring the proper performance of their duties by entities empowered to settle consumer disputes out of court. The Free and Solidarni Members' Circle will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u325	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for these questions. A brief word of reflection. Today we are talking about something new in this bill, we are talking about an alternative consumer dispute resolution system. Although we have heard that you have spoken about the system, I would like to say that there is no such system. It is only by implementing the Directive that this law introduces a system and a way of managing a consumer complaint. I would like to thank all the Members who worked in committees, subcommittees, because it was really difficult to process this law, because today such a substitute for these eligible entities, ADR entities somewhere in public entities we have and these resources had to be used to tailor a new system. It didn't always work, hence the hard work in the subcommittee, so that this patchwork system can be assembled together so that it can function. Let me now answer your questions. The first question concerned payment. Indeed, in principle, the Act provides that consumers are not to be charged a fee. This procedure is free of charge, although the rules of procedure may provide for the collection of a fee from the consumer. The Member asked for a reason. This condition is in the bill. She's designed in a negative way, but she is. Such a fee must not hinder the consumer's access to the system. According to our analysis, the fee for identifying such a dispute should not exceed 30% and 50%. I stress that the Act provides for a certain control, coordination of the President of the OMC, so if the rules of procedure provide for such fees, these fees will be subject to scrutiny by the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. Today we know that to a large extent the system will be built on the basis of public entities that will be created at the Electronic Communications Office, the Energy Regulatory Office, the Financial Spokesperson, the Financial Supervision Commission, the Civil Aviation Authority, the Railway Transport Office and the Trade Inspection Office. Today, these public bodies declare, in principle, that such charges will not be charged. The second question concerned requirements which would be brought before those who settle disputes in such eligible entities. The law actually creates these requirements in a general way. I would stress that the law does not prejudge that this form of out-of-court dispute resolution must only be mediation, if only arbitration, if only amicable judicial proceedings. This range is very wide, so these requirements will also depend on the form that will be chosen by the individual authorized entity. If the entity chooses mediation, then the person who decides in such a entity will, of course, have to comply with the requirements of the Code of Civil Procedure, i.e. here more soft predispositions... If the entity chooses a decision which is binding, those competences will probably have to be closer to knowledge of the law and to settlement. Finally, it will be the bodies empowered in their rules of procedure to determine these competences and, as I mentioned earlier, these rules will be subject to monitoring of coordination checks by the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. The third question concerned 90 days. Yes, it is the deadline for the directive. There was also such a question, Madam President, that it was not too short. Let me put it this way: from our experience, we know that the commercial inspection, which the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection directly supervises, meets such deadlines, and this is an average period for resolving a consumer dispute, whether in the form of mediation or in an amicable form. The fourth question, which is very important, concerns consumers' mouths. Ladies and gentlemen, there is a reason why I am talking about a system for resolving certain consumer issues, consumer complaints, consumer problems. This is a system. On the one hand, These are the powers of the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, on the other hand, the spokespersons who provide individual assistance to consumers in the field, and the third element of this system is the alternative dispute resolution system, which is the system we are talking about today. These systems will be complementary, and judging by the number of consumer complaints affecting your offices, the Members' offices, I think I am able to say with full responsibility that no one will lack work: neither the spokespersons nor those who will settle consumer disputes in these ADR entities. The Member's fifth question concerned the relationship between the new ADR system and the cases dealt with there to the number of cases dealt with in the courts. Here's a curiosity answer, because, ladies and gentlemen, from our research... We have conducted studies at all on the tendency of consumers to submit to disputes in this alternative system, and these studies show that this system is not an alternative to the court at all. Why? Because today consumers with cases where the value of the subject matter of the dispute is not high, simply do not go to court. You do not go to court with a complaint of shoes, with a complaint of badly performed repair and construction service. The court is dealing with a polysolocata case, a franc, so today we do not have such data, although I assume that when this system is reviewed after some time, such data will appear. The sixth question was... [[The equality of the pages.]] ...equality of the parties of the trader and the consumer. Thank you, sir. In my view, this equality is more guaranteed in the new ADR system than before. So far we have a consumer who is lonely, unless he knows that there is a consumer spokesperson, and then he uses his help, and on the other hand we have a large entrepreneur with a staff of lawyers. It seems to me that what will happen in the alternative dispute resolution centers, i.e. a mediator or an amicable judiciary, or someone who will be independent here... This balance between the consumer and the trader will be preserved. [[That's all.]] Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u329	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The proposal submitted to the High House for a law amending the Military Property Agency Act, print No 841, provides for the extension of the powers of the Military Property Agency to provide services to organisational units subordinate to or supervised by the Minister for National Defence. From the existing provision in this regard - Article. 53th Act on the Military Property Agency - it appears that the Military Property Agency can provide services exclusively to the Minister of National Defence, thus at present it cannot provide such services to military units. The Agency and its subsidiaries could provide specialised critical infrastructure services. This is extremely important in view of the current threats of our country. For security reasons, a better solution is one that involves the provision of services for e.g. ammunition warehouses or other elements of military infrastructure by an entity under the full control of the Minister for National Defence. It shall be stressed that in accordance with Article. Article 14 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the Act of 22 June 1995 on the Accommodation of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland, which expired on 1 October 2015 pursuant to Article. 107 of the Act on the Military Property Agency, the Minister of National Defence, may conclude agreements with the Military Housing Agency for the provision of services not only to the Ministry of National Defence, but also to organisational units subordinate to or supervised by the Minister of National Defence. In view of the fact that the Military Property Agency was to perform the tasks of the Military and Military Property Agency after the merger, it is necessary for the Military Property Agency to be able to perform services and works, as well as for the repair of organisational units subordinate to or supervised by the Minister for National Defence, as well as directly to the Minister for National Defence. In addition, the amendment of the Act on the Military Property Agency gives the possibility, on the basis of a decision of the Minister of National Defence or the Minister responsible for Home Affairs, to transfer or dispose of military equipment or dual-use items and arms, as well as to dispose of military equipment or dual-use items and of strategic importance for the maintenance of international peace and security in the hands of organisational units subordinate to the Minister of National Defence or the Minister competent for Home Affairs. The proposed amendments are designed to reduce the time involved in deciding whether to transfer or dispose of military equipment or dual-use items free of charge. Previous practice in this area indicates that negotiation processes related to the conclusion of international agreements are too time-consuming and that the situation sometimes requires immediate action. The maintenance of decision-making procedures and processes may lead to the loss of the ability of foreign entities to dispose of military equipment or dual-use items and sometimes to take over a given contract or market. The amendment of the Act also includes the introduction of solutions that will improve the functioning of the Agency, as well as simplify and reform the principles of entrusting key posts with the Military Property Agency. The proposed solutions in this respect are similar to those adopted for other executive agencies, e.g. Agricultural Real Estate Agency, Agricultural Market Agency. The proposed provisions provide that the President of the Military Property Agency will appoint and dismiss the President of the Council of Ministers at the request of the Minister for National Defence. The proposed solution implies the scope of the Agency's tasks for defence and state security purposes, since the Agency carries out strategic tasks from the point of view of the national defence department. Resignation from the holding of competitions for the position of President and Deputy President shall not imply resignation from the requirement of those persons appointed to such important positions to have the qualifications laid down in the Military Property Agency Act. This is defined in Article. 11 laws. The fact that the Act of 10 July 2015 on the Military Property Agency contains a provision allowing the Minister of National Defence to appoint the first President of the Agency and its deputies without a competition. It's about art. 123th Military Property Agency Act. It is also proposed to simplify the procedures for the appointment of deputy presidents of the agencies and directors of regional branches. The Deputy Chairman of the Military Property Agency appointed and will be dismissed by the Minister of National Defence at the request of the President of the Agency, while the Directors of Regional Branches will be appointed by the President of the Agency. In view of the solution proposed in the draft law, it should be assumed that the proposed regulation will undoubtedly contribute to improving the functioning of the Military Property Agency. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-21-sejm-26-1.u389	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the Government's draft law amending certain laws in order to facilitate the fight against infectious animal diseases, prints 832 and 865. The first reading of the draft law took place at today's meeting of the Sejm. Today, too, the committee has examined the government bill. The aim of the Act is to increase the effectiveness of the fight against infectious animal diseases by introducing new obligations, inter alia. for animal owners, operators of the hunting industry, as well as the equipment of the Veterinary Inspection to impose additional orders, prohibitions and restrictions in relation to the eradication of infectious animal diseases, as well as the introduction of administrative sanctions for non-compliance with such prohibitions, orders and restrictions or non-compliance with obligations relating to the identification and registration of animals. Due to the presence of African swine fever in Poland, the adoption of the proposed solutions is a need for a moment and this view was dominated by the work of the committee. During the examination of the project by the committee, several amendments were tabled to the draft law. These included, in particular, allowing wild boars to be shot in health not only in hunting circuits but also in protected areas, such as, for example, wild boars. National parks, making it possible for the tenants or managers of hunting circuits or the Polish Hunting Union to pay back costs in a flat-rate form for carrying out sanitary fire. A lump sum should be granted in 70% to hunters and 30% to tenants, managers of hunting circuits or the Polish Hunting Union. The entire lump sum would be paid by a district veterinarian. They also concerned the extension of the scope of operators registered in the livestock register marked with entities included in the system of identification and registration of animals other than the seat of the herd, slaughterhouse, processing plant or incineration plant; amendments to the existing legislation concerning the employment of district veterinarians and their alternates in order to ensure the proper supervision of the provincial veterinarian of the district veterinarian and, consequently, the effective control of infectious animal diseases and the spread of such diseases. The operation of inspections in specific situations, and this is the need to combat African swine fever, requires immediate decision-making, efficient coordination, ensuring the smooth and rapid implementation of the instructions given, as well as a rapid response to the threat. The Commission has examined and accepted these amendments. However, the proposed amendment was not accepted in order to increase the liability of the tenants or managers of the hunting circuit in the event of a failure to order a sanitary discharge issued by the district veterinarian and sanctions in the form of a termination of the agreement for the lease of the hunting circuit. Following the adoption of the amendments, the Commission has adopted the entire draft law and recommends the adoption of a draft law presented to ladies and gentlemen in print No 865. The High Sejm announces the attached project. Thank you very much.

On 18 September 2016, celebrations commemorating the 90th anniversary of the creation of the Volunteer Fire Service unit in Bochotnica took place. Bochotnica is an ancient town located in the western part of the Lublin Voivodeship, in the Puławy district, in the municipality of Kazimierz Dolny, on the rivers Bystra and Wisła. It is one of the oldest settlement centers in Małopolska, already inhabited between the 6th century and the 10th century. Today Bochotnica is a local tourist and service centre, as well as an important communication hub. It has an extensive rest and night base, thanks to which it serves mainly as a tourist and leisure function during the summer season. During the ceremony there was a collection of CSR units, a Mass during which the flag given to the unit was dedicated. In addition to firefighters from several units of the municipality Kazimierz Dolny took part in the ceremony. representatives of district and communal authorities, fire chiefs of various levels, police officers, well-deserved firefighters' mates, local social activists and local community. I, too, was very pleased to accept the invitation to this ceremony. The start of the CSR in Bochotnica dates back to 1926. It was then, on 26 May, that the organization of Mon was registered in the District Fire Association in Puławy and at the commune of Celejów. Volunteer Fire Department in Bochotnica. This decision was preceded by repeated meetings and findings of a group of enthusiasts, chaired by Bolesław Staroń, school head, a light man, a highly active, inspiring environment to face still new challenges. The founding members' membership consisted of 25 men. In the middle of the village, she allocated a 50-acre plot of land on which the farm system was built for the village's guard. The main equipment of the fire unit was a prop cart, two horses, two-wheeled water barrels with a capacity of 400 l, a ladder, a suction-pressing hand piss and barefoot. Each firefighter involved in the firefight was equipped with a combat uniform, a helmet, a seat belt and a hatchet. The house had an alarm signature and steel gongs. An effective alarm element was also the sound of a trumpet with a specific modulation. Currently, the Bochotnica TSO is a modern unit in the national fire protection and fire protection system with modern equipment, including a well-equipped medium tonnage car on the Renault Midlum 4x4 chassis designed for technical rescue and fire extinguisher and equipped with equipment for this purpose. Paramedics are equipped with personal protective equipment against various variants encountered during rescue operations, communication systems, rescue tools, saws and saws. Currently, the list of members and the composition of the Board of TSOs Bochotnica includes 16 members of ordinary members, 2 members of honour, 11 supporting members, 10 members of the youth fire team, 9 members of the Board and 4 members of the audit committee. For all firefighters who have earned their service in the service of the CSR in Bochotnica over these 90 years, I would like to offer my warm thanks and congratulations. To those who have left, I salute you. I wish my current fellow Members the happiness, happiness in their actions, satisfaction in their service and respect from the local community. Thank you very much.
